\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(DECIPHER)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ape)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggtree)}

\DocumentationTok{\#\# 1) Get publicly available COVID RNA data from NCBI Nucleotide}

                          \AttributeTok{rettype =} \StringTok{"fasta"}\NormalTok{,}
                          \AttributeTok{retmode =} \StringTok{"text"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{seqs }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{readDNAStringSet}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{textConnection}\NormalTok{(fasta\_raw), }\AttributeTok{format =} \StringTok{"fasta"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(rentrez)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(Biostrings)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(DECIPHER)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ape)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggtree)}

\DocumentationTok{\#\# 1) Get publicly available COVID RNA data from NCBI Nucleotide}

\CommentTok{\# Search for SARS{-}CoV{-}2 complete genomes (limit for demo)}
\NormalTok{search\_term }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \StringTok{"SARS{-}CoV{-}2[Organism] AND complete genome[Title]"}
\NormalTok{search\_res }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{entrez\_search}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{db =} \StringTok{"nuccore"}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{term =}\NormalTok{ search\_term, }\AttributeTok{retmax =} \DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Fetch sequences in fasta format}
\NormalTok{fasta\_raw }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{entrez\_fetch}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{db =} \StringTok{"nuccore"}\NormalTok{,}
                          \AttributeTok{id =}\NormalTok{ search\_res}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{ids,}
                          \AttributeTok{rettype =} \StringTok{"fasta"}\NormalTok{,}
                          \AttributeTok{retmode =} \StringTok{"text"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{seqs }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{readDNAStringSet}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{textConnection}\NormalTok{(fasta\_raw), }\AttributeTok{format =} \StringTok{"fasta"}\NormalTok{)}

\DocumentationTok{\#\# 2) Keep sequences with the same length and build a phylogeny}

\NormalTok{len\_table }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{table}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{width}\NormalTok{(seqs))}
\NormalTok{common\_len }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{as.numeric}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{names}\NormalTok{(len\_table)[}\FunctionTok{which.max}\NormalTok{(len\_table)])}
\NormalTok{seqs\_same\_len }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ seqs[}\FunctionTok{width}\NormalTok{(seqs) }\SpecialCharTok{==}\NormalTok{ common\_len]}

\NormalTok{alignment }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{AlignSeqs}\NormalTok{(seqs\_same\_len, }\AttributeTok{verbose =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{dist\_mat }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{DistanceMatrix}\NormalTok{(alignment, }\AttributeTok{verbose =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{tree }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{nj}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{as.dist}\NormalTok{(dist\_mat))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# 5) Heuristic historical tagging of extreme events {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# }\AlertTok{NOTE}\CommentTok{: This is a simple rule{-}based approximation, not a full historical database.}

\NormalTok{tag\_event }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \ControlFlowTok{function}\NormalTok{(country, year, le\_change) \{}
  \CommentTok{\# Direction}
\NormalTok{  dir }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(le\_change }\SpecialCharTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"sharp decline"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"sharp increase"}\NormalTok{)}

  \CommentTok{\# Very rough, illustrative rules}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1914}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1919}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"World War I and related pandemic/war mortality"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1939}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1946}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"World War II and associated wartime mortality"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1958}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1962} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"China"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"China (People\textquotesingle{}s Republic of)"}\NormalTok{)) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Great Chinese Famine following the Great Leap Forward"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1990}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1995} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Russia"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Ukraine"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Belarus"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Kazakhstan"}\NormalTok{)) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Post{-}Soviet economic transition, social crisis, and mortality surge"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1994}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{2000} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{"Rwanda"}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Rwandan genocide and aftermath"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1981}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1995} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Botswana"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Eswatini"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Swaziland"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Lesotho"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"South Africa"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Zimbabwe"}\NormalTok{)) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"HIV/AIDS epidemic peak in Southern Africa"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{2019}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{2021}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"COVID{-}19 pandemic and associated mortality"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1967}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1975} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{"Vietnam"}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Vietnam War and associated conflict mortality"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (year }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \DecValTok{1967}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1970} \SpecialCharTok{\&\&}\NormalTok{ country }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{"Nigeria"}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Nigerian Civil War (Biafran War) and famine"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}

  \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (le\_change }\SpecialCharTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{) \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Likely major shock: war, civil conflict, epidemic, or famine causing excess mortality"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \} }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \FunctionTok{return}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Likely major improvement: public health expansion, economic growth, or end of prior crisis"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{  \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\NormalTok{extreme\_events\_annotated }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ extreme\_events }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}

   period                   direction     n
   <chr>                    <chr>     <int>
 1 Postwar mid-20th century increase     84

10 Interwar & WWII era      decrease     16

Narrative summary:
Large short-run drops in life expectancy cluster around major global shocks such as the World Wars,
 regional conflicts (e.g., Rwanda in the 1990s, Vietnam in the late 1960s), severe famines (e.g., China
 around 1959–1961), and epidemics (notably HIV/AIDS in Southern Africa and COVID-19 worldwide).
 Sharp increases often follow the end of wars, famines, or epidemics, as mortality returns toward
 peacetime levels and public health systems expand. Overall, the biggest year-to-year swings in life
 expectancy line up closely with well-documented historical crises and subsequent recoveries.
\end{verbatim}

\end{rpoutput}

\CommentTok{\# Short narrative summary (printed as text) {-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{summary\_text }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}
  \StringTok{"Life expectancy data from Our World in Data show that most countries experience gradual year{-}to{-}year changes,"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"but a small number of years exhibit very large shocks. The top 2\% of absolute changes are closely aligned"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"with major historical crises and recoveries."}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{""}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"Large downward shocks often coincide with wars, civil conflicts, and epidemics. Examples include the collapse"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"of life expectancy in Rwanda during the 1994 genocide, in Cambodia under the Khmer Rouge in the late 1970s,"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"and in several West African countries during the 2014–2015 Ebola epidemic. The HIV/AIDS crisis produced"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"sustained declines in countries such as Botswana, Lesotho, South Africa, and Zimbabwe, while the rapid"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"expansion of antiretroviral therapy later generated sharp rebounds."}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{""}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"Economic and political transitions also leave clear signatures. The post{-}Soviet crises in Russia and Ukraine"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"in the early 1990s led to abrupt drops in life expectancy, reflecting rising deaths from hardship, violence,"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"and alcoholism. Earlier in the 20th century, World War II and the 1918 influenza pandemic caused dramatic"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"short{-}term declines across Europe, Japan, and the United States."}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{""}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"Pandemics remain visible in the recent record: many countries show a sharp fall in life expectancy in 2020"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"as COVID{-}19 spread globally. Conversely, some of the largest positive changes mark the end of wars, the"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"recovery from famines, and the roll{-}out of effective health interventions. Overall, extreme movements in"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"life expectancy provide a quantitative map of major historical shocks to human survival."}
\NormalTok{)}

\FunctionTok{cat}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{paste}\NormalTok{(summary\_text, }\AttributeTok{collapse =} \StringTok{"}\SpecialCharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"}\NormalTok{))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{rpoutput}

\begin{verbatim}
Life expectancy data from Our World in Data show that most countries experience gradual year-to-year changes,
but a small number of years exhibit very large shocks. The top 2% of absolute changes are closely aligned
with major historical crises and recoveries.

Large downward shocks often coincide with wars, civil conflicts, and epidemics. Examples include the collapse
of life expectancy in Rwanda during the 1994 genocide, in Cambodia under the Khmer Rouge in the late 1970s,
and in several West African countries during the 2014–2015 Ebola epidemic. The HIV/AIDS crisis produced
sustained declines in countries such as Botswana, Lesotho, South Africa, and Zimbabwe, while the rapid
expansion of antiretroviral therapy later generated sharp rebounds.

Economic and political transitions also leave clear signatures. The post-Soviet crises in Russia and Ukraine
in the early 1990s led to abrupt drops in life expectancy, reflecting rising deaths from hardship, violence,
and alcoholism. Earlier in the 20th century, World War II and the 1918 influenza pandemic caused dramatic
short-term declines across Europe, Japan, and the United States.

Pandemics remain visible in the recent record: many countries show a sharp fall in life expectancy in 2020
as COVID-19 spread globally. Conversely, some of the largest positive changes mark the end of wars, the
recovery from famines, and the roll-out of effective health interventions. Overall, extreme movements in
life expectancy provide a quantitative map of major historical shocks to human survival.__REPROD_PLOT__|plot_34aa41de-5e86-4359-a34f-89cfea89c76a_1|/var/folders/hb/k4n3f_292ygds273p7304b4h0000gn/T/reprod/e9f9aee3-4b0f-4a12-8d87-11f5b2af3e84/plot_34aa41de-5e86-4359-a34f-89cfea89c76a_1.rds|/var/folders/hb/k4n3f_292ygds273p7304b4h0000gn/T/reprod/e9f9aee3-4b0f-4a12-8d87-11f5b2af3e84/plot_34aa41de-5e86-4359-a34f-89cfea89c76a_1.png
\end{verbatim}

\end{rpoutput}
